# The Evaluation Tool, Its Tasks and the Move from task-check.ps1 (Plan 6A) Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Build `:tools:eval`, the Kotlin program on the PC that runs the agent's tasks over adb and replaces `scripts\task-check.ps1`, with the six D3 tasks, their stand-ins and the 14 injection fixtures in it; move `scripts\a71-agent.ps1` to it; delete `scripts\task-check.ps1` and its tests; and run the stand-ins, the fixtures and a rehearsal on kestrel-api37 with the scripted model.

**Architecture:** `tools/eval` is a plain Kotlin JVM module (`kestrel.jvm.library` with Gradle's `application` plugin) that depends on no other Kestrel module: it reaches Kestrel only through adb and the debug hooks. Its layers are small and each has its own tests against a fake device: `device` (adb, the device guard, the guarded shell with its retry rule, the dump readers, the test photos), `hook` (the hooks' broadcast and one function per hook), `run` (the restores, the call guard, the runner, the run line, the preflight and the live gate), `task` (the typed setup steps and checks, and the tasks themselves), `report` (the results files and `report.json`) and `command` (the command line). `scripts\eval.ps1` builds the tool's installed copy only when its sources changed, and starts it in the repository's folder.

**Tech Stack:** Kotlin 2.4.21 on the JVM (JDK 21 from Android Studio, target 17), kotlinx.serialization 1.13.0, `java.awt` and `javax.imageio` for the test photos, JUnit 4.13.2, Gradle's `application` plugin, PowerShell 7.3 and Windows PowerShell 5.1, Pester 6.2.0 and PSScriptAnalyzer 1.25.0.

## Global Constraints

- Package and application ID `dev.kestrel`; licence GPL-3.0-or-later. Nothing new ships in the app. `:tools:eval` uses only `kotlinx-serialization-json` and JUnit, which the version catalog and `gradle/verification-metadata.xml` already have, and depends on no other Kestrel module (design D2).
- The privacy audit scans `:tools:eval` as it scans every `kestrel.jvm.library` module: no `java.net` connection, no `URL` read (a resource is read as a stream), no OkHttp. Plan 6B adds the one network rule of the tool, for PCAPdroid's download.
- No request to any provider's API in this plan: every run uses the scripted model, and no step runs `--live`. Live runs need no sitting from this plan on (design D1), but plan 6B's baseline is the first that uses them.
- Never print, log or write into a result the owner's words, screen text, a notification, an SMS, an e-mail, a contact, a prompt, a reply, a tool's arguments or a value of `corpus\owner\a71-config.json`: the run line holds only what the hooks answered, a warning goes through `Redactions.forConsole`, and the tool never reads the key `pin` (design D2, D5 and D6).
- The run line stays exactly task-check.ps1's: `task=<name> run=<i> result=<pass|fail|skipped> end=<EndReason|none> check=<pass|fail|skipped> steps= workMs= credits=<n|unknown> model= in= out= cacheRead= cacheWrite= reason=<ok|timeout|check|end|untestable|limit|confirm|call|stopped|setup|stalled>`, and a results file's line is `<yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss>\t<step>\t<text>` in UTF-8 without a BOM (design D6).
- Every change on a device pushes its restore before the change, with the value read before it; a restore that fails names what is left and how to undo it by hand (design D3, D4 and D9). Tests use only test data: the aliases' keys are refused when the owner has them, the fixture contacts live in the local account `kestrel-test`, and the test photos in `Pictures/KestrelTest`.
- `:tools:eval` accepts only kestrel-api37 and the Galaxy A71 and refuses the Galaxy S23+ always; every shell command checks the device in the same `adb shell` call, reads are tried 3 times and a command that acts runs once (design D3).
- Test only on kestrel-api37 (`emulator-5554`), with the emulator's check in the same command as each device command: `. scripts\lib\Kestrel.ps1; Use-KestrelToolchain; if ("$(& (Get-Adb) -s emulator-5554 emu avd name)" -notmatch '^kestrel-api37\b') { Write-Error 'emulator-5554 is not kestrel-api37' -ErrorAction Stop }`. If the check fails, or no emulator runs, start it with `scripts\start-emulator.ps1`, check the serial that it prints, and use that serial. Never use the AVD `zoran` or the S23+; no step of this plan uses the A71.
- kestrel-api37 holds the owner's own Google account and Kestrel's Copilot profile: no step uninstalls Kestrel there, clears its data, or signs an account in or out. Each device step checks before and after it that `dumpsys account` counts the same Google accounts and that `scripts\hook.ps1 -Serial emulator-5554 -Command provider-standin` answers `profile=copilot`; `:tools:eval` checks the same around each of its commands on kestrel-api37.
- The controller holds `.superpowers\sdd\emulator.lock` for each task that uses the emulator (process rule 9); never create, wait for or delete it. Only one Gradle build at a time (process rule 8b).
- Run Gradle only through `scripts\gradle.ps1`, and stop it with `scripts\gradle.ps1 --stop` at the end of each task that builds. Install only through `scripts\install.ps1`. Run device work only through the scripts that AGENTS.md names; from Task 11 on, that list names `scripts\eval.ps1` in place of `scripts\task-check.ps1`.
- No step ever places a call or answers "yes, call" (process rule 10): the runner ends a call that shows with the end-call key and fails the run.
- Scripts run in Windows PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7, or declare `#Requires -Version 7.3` as `hook.ps1` does; `scripts\test-scripts.ps1` passes after every script change.
- The JVM tests of `:tools:eval` run on CI's Ubuntu too: no test needs Windows, a device, a font or a network.
- The repository uses LF line endings.
- This plan is `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-23-eval-6a-tool.md`. A plan is frozen once committed; a later difference goes into `.superpowers/sdd/progress.md`.
- Every commit message ends with the line `Co-authored-by: Copilot <223556219+Copilot@users.noreply.github.com>`.

D-numbers refer to the design `.superpowers/sdd/plan-6-design.md`. Code comments call it "design D… of sub-project 6", and sub-project 5's design "design D… of sub-project 5".

## Before you start

- The code is `origin/main` at `a2cdc1b` (the design's spec commit), or later commits of this plan.
- The counts at the start of this plan are those at `a2cdc1b`. The controller gives their values in each task's brief: the Pester tests T₀ in each shell and the `:app` JVM tests A₀ (with the debug-only JVM tests of `app/src/testDebug`); `:tools:eval` has no tests yet (0). Each task gives its counts as "N + k", where N is the count before the task; before each task, compare the counts with the ones that the earlier tasks left. If a count differs, stop and ask the controller.
- Before each task, check that every text that the task replaces exists, and exists once, in its file. If a text is missing, stop and ask the controller.
- Before each task, run `git status --short`. If a file that the task changes already shows changes that are not the task's own, stop and ask the controller, because the task's `git add` would commit them too.
- Tasks 10 and 12 use the emulator; the controller says in their briefs that it holds the emulator's lock. If it does not, ask before the device steps.
- In PowerShell, quote a list of tasks for `scripts\eval.ps1`, as in `--tasks 'stand-in-hotspot,stand-in-read'`: unquoted, PowerShell passes `a,b` on as two arguments.

## Not in this plan

- Plan 6B: the agent's tools `set_slider`, `add_contact` and `delete_contact`; the 23 new tasks with their scripts, steps and checks; the hooks of D17 other than `agent-memory -Has` (`agent-task -Digits`, `screen-tap -Contains` and `-Long`, `network-allowlist`); `NotificationActivity`'s marker; the deletion of the test e-mails in Gmail (read-email's too, design D9); `egress-setup`, the capture, `PcapReader` and the egress check; the whole `report.json`, `eval\baseline.json`, `compare` with `--rerun`, `--record-baseline`; the live suites and the baseline; AGENTS.md's sentence on PCAPdroid.
- Plan 6C: datamarking and `injection --datamarking`, and the live injection runs. Plan 6D: the network log and `docs\release-checklist.md`.
- Any live run, any provider call, and any step on the A71.

## Changes to the design that this plan makes

- Task 1's command line knows no command yet; `list` comes with Task 9's commands, because it lists the catalogue of Tasks 5 to 8. Task 1's smoke test runs `scripts\eval.ps1` without arguments, which builds the tool and ends with the usage and exit code 2.
- `scripts\gradle.ps1` refuses every task whose name holds "install" unless `ANDROID_SERIAL` names a test device (`Get-DeviceTaskArgument`), so it refused `:tools:eval:installDist`, which builds the tool on this computer and reaches no device. Task 1 lets `installDist` through that check, with a Pester case.
- The design's two dry-run forms of `stand-in-messenger-late` are two tasks: `stand-in-messenger-late` and `stand-in-messenger-late-reworded`, which differ only in their script. A results line names its task, so the two forms stay apart in `runs.txt`, in `report.json` and in a71-agent.ps1's summaries.
- `run` without `--live` always runs the task's form without a model, with `--runs` runs; `--dry-run` runs it once, which is what a71-agent.ps1's dry run asks for. A run line of a D3 task's dry run names the stand-in that ran, as task-check.ps1's did.
- Task 7 copies the scripts of `scripts\tasks` into the tool's resources, and Task 11 deletes `scripts\tasks` with task-check.ps1, because a71-agent.ps1 runs them through task-check.ps1 until Task 10.
- `OwnerEmails` (design D4 says that it leaves the owner's addresses set; D7 says "with a restore"): when test mode had no addresses, its restore empties the list again, because a71-agent.ps1's `Set-OwnerNames` stops a sitting when test mode has addresses; when it had some, they stay, as D4 says, because the hook cannot read them back. No task of this plan uses it; plan 6B's email-self does.
- The tool's `waze-home` sets the A71's test location itself (design D4's `TestLocation`), so Task 10 removes a71-agent.ps1's own test location around that task; otherwise two senders of fixes would run at once.
- The preflight checks that Kestrel's accessibility service is on and refuses when it is off, instead of turning it on: turning it on replaces the owner's list of accessibility services, which a71-agent.ps1 and `connected-test.ps1` save and put back themselves. When Kestrel does not listen within 90 s before the first run, the command is a refusal (exit code 2, no run line), where task-check.ps1 wrote `stopped` lines.
- The live gate's restore of the live session runs before the restore of test mode, so that a live session is never open without test mode (design D1 says that it is the first restore pushed; it is the first of the gate's own).
- A rehearsal compares, after the restores, the values that each step read just before its change (`Setup.probe`), because a step that reads a value needs the run's values that the earlier steps set, such as the test name.
- The test photos are cards of one colour with as many white bars as their number, instead of a drawn digit, so that they need no font on CI; their EXIF segment is Photos.ps1's, byte for byte.
- `AccountGuard` reads the default profile through `provider-standin`, which a stopped or missing Kestrel does not answer; it then compares only the count of Google accounts. Kestrel is started before the first read.
- A lost answer of a fixture contact's data row fails the run instead of reading the row back; the restore deletes every contact of the account `kestrel-test` anyway.
- read-email's test e-mail stays in the inbox until plan 6B adds its deletion (6B's Task 8), as task-check.ps1 left it; Task 12's rehearsal leaves read-email out for that reason.
- `hotspot` runs on kestrel-api37 only (design D8), so a71-agent.ps1's call of it on the A71 writes a skipped line (`reason=untestable`).
- AGENTS.md gets the sentence on PCAPdroid with plan 6B, which adds `egress-setup`.

### Task 1: The module, `scripts\eval.ps1` and its out-of-date rule

**Files:**
- Create: `tools/eval/build.gradle.kts`, `tools/eval/src/main/kotlin/dev/kestrel/eval/Main.kt`, `tools/eval/src/main/kotlin/dev/kestrel/eval/Cli.kt`, `tools/eval/src/test/kotlin/dev/kestrel/eval/CliTest.kt`, `scripts/eval.ps1`, `scripts/tests/Eval.Tests.ps1`
- Modify: `settings.gradle.kts`, `.gitignore`, `scripts/lib/Kestrel.ps1` (`Get-DeviceTaskArgument`), `scripts/tests/Kestrel.Tests.ps1` (one case)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: the convention plugin `kestrel.jvm.library` (Kotlin JVM, Java 17, the privacy audit), `libs.kotlinx.serialization.json`, `libs.junit`; `scripts\gradle.ps1`, `Use-KestrelToolchain` and `Get-DeviceTaskArgument` of `scripts\lib\Kestrel.ps1`.
- Produces: the module `:tools:eval` with `mainClass = dev.kestrel.eval.MainKt` and `applicationName = "eval"`, so that `:tools:eval:installDist` writes `tools\eval\build\install\eval\bin\eval.bat` and `lib\eval.jar`; `object Cli { const val EXIT_PASSED = 0; const val EXIT_FAILED = 1; const val EXIT_REFUSED = 2; val USAGE: String; fun run(args: List<String>, out: PrintStream): Int }`, which Task 9 replaces with `class Cli(environment: Environment) { fun run(args: List<String>): Int }` and the same constants; `scripts\eval.ps1 <arguments>` with its helpers `Get-NewestSource([string]$Root)`, `Test-EvalStale([string]$Root)` (compares the newest source with the stamp `tools\eval\build\eval-built.stamp`, not with `eval.jar`, which `installDist` leaves as it was when the jar does not change), `Invoke-Gradle([string[]]$Arguments)` (returns the exit code) and `Update-EvalTool([string]$Root)` (writes the stamp after a build that succeeded, and returns whether the installed copy is current); `.gitignore`'s `/eval-results/` and `/eval/local/`.

Counts: `:tools:eval` JVM 0 + 2 (`CliTest`); Pester N + 8 in each shell (`Eval.Tests.ps1` 7, `Kestrel.Tests.ps1` 1); `:app` unchanged.

- [ ] **Step 1: Add the module to the build**

In `settings.gradle.kts`, replace:

```kotlin
include(":tools:catalog-sync")
```

with:

```kotlin
include(":tools:catalog-sync")
include(":tools:eval")
```

Create `tools/eval/build.gradle.kts`:

```kotlin
plugins {
    id("kestrel.jvm.library")
    id("org.jetbrains.kotlin.plugin.serialization")
    application
}

// :tools:eval runs the agent's task suite on the PC over adb (design spec, section 16.5). It depends on no other Kestrel
// module, because it reaches Kestrel only through adb and the debug hooks, so a change in the app never rebuilds it.
// scripts\eval.ps1 builds its installed copy with :tools:eval:installDist and starts build\install\eval\bin\eval.bat.
application {
    mainClass.set("dev.kestrel.eval.MainKt")
    applicationName = "eval"
}

dependencies {
    implementation(libs.kotlinx.serialization.json)
    testImplementation(libs.junit)
}
```

In `.gitignore`, replace:

```text
# The release signing key, which lives only in the owner's folders and in GitHub's secrets.
```

with:

```text
# The results of :tools:eval (scripts\eval.ps1), which can hold personal data, and its local keys (design spec, section 16.5)
/eval-results/
/eval/local/
# The release signing key, which lives only in the owner's folders and in GitHub's secrets.
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Write the failing test of the command line**

Create `tools/eval/src/test/kotlin/dev/kestrel/eval/CliTest.kt`:

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.eval

import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
import org.junit.Test
import java.io.ByteArrayOutputStream
import java.io.PrintStream

/** The first form of the command line: the usage, and exit code 2 for anything it does not know (design D2 of sub-project 6). */
class CliTest {
    @Test
    fun withoutACommandItPrintsTheUsageAndRefuses() {
        val bytes = ByteArrayOutputStream()

        val code = Cli.run(emptyList(), PrintStream(bytes, true, Charsets.UTF_8))

        assertEquals(Cli.EXIT_REFUSED, code)
        assertTrue(bytes.toString(Charsets.UTF_8).startsWith("Usage: scripts\\eval.ps1 <command> [options]"))
    }

    @Test
    fun anUnknownCommandIsNamedAndRefused() {
        val bytes = ByteArrayOutputStream()

        val code = Cli.run(listOf("fly"), PrintStream(bytes, true, Charsets.UTF_8))

        assertEquals(Cli.EXIT_REFUSED, code)
        assertTrue(bytes.toString(Charsets.UTF_8).startsWith("Unknown command fly."))
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the first form of the command line**

Create `tools/eval/src/main/kotlin/dev/kestrel/eval/Main.kt`:

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.eval

import kotlin.system.exitProcess

/** Starts :tools:eval with the arguments that `scripts\eval.ps1` passes on, and ends with the command's exit code. */
fun main(args: Array<String>) {
    exitProcess(Cli.run(args.toList(), System.out))
}
```

Create `tools/eval/src/main/kotlin/dev/kestrel/eval/Cli.kt`:

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.eval

import java.io.PrintStream

/**
 * The command line of :tools:eval (design D2 of sub-project 6). This first form knows no command yet: it prints the
 * usage and refuses, with exit code 2, as every refusal before a run does. Plan 6A's Task 9 adds the commands.
 */
object Cli {
    const val EXIT_PASSED = 0
    const val EXIT_FAILED = 1
    const val EXIT_REFUSED = 2

    val USAGE: String = """
        |Usage: scripts\eval.ps1 <command> [options]
        |  run --device <serial> --task <name> [--runs n] [--live] [--model id] [--name first-name] [--dry-run] [--results file]
        |  suite --device <serial> [--live] [--runs n] [--tasks a,b]
        |  injection --device <serial> [--live] [--runs n]
        |  rehearse --device <serial> [--tasks a,b]
        |  list [--device <serial>]
    """.trimMargin()

    fun run(args: List<String>, out: PrintStream): Int {
        if (args.isNotEmpty()) out.println("Unknown command ${args.first()}.")
        out.println(USAGE)
        return EXIT_REFUSED
    }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Write the failing Pester tests of the launcher and of the device guard**

Create `scripts/tests/Eval.Tests.ps1`:

```powershell
# Tests of scripts\eval.ps1's out-of-date rule and of its rebuild (design D2 of sub-project 6). The script needs Gradle
# and a device, so the tests load only its helper functions from the script's syntax tree, and build a small tree of files
# with their own write times in the test drive. scripts\test-scripts.ps1 runs these tests in Windows PowerShell 5.1 and in
# PowerShell 7.
[Diagnostics.CodeAnalysis.SuppressMessageAttribute('PSUseDeclaredVarsMoreThanAssignments', '',
    Justification = 'Pester shares the variables of BeforeAll with the It blocks.')]
param()

BeforeAll {
    $tokens = $null
    $errors = $null
    $tree = [System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile("$PSScriptRoot\..\eval.ps1", [ref]$tokens, [ref]$errors)
    $helpers = @('Get-NewestSource', 'Test-EvalStale', 'Invoke-Gradle', 'Update-EvalTool')
    $functions = $tree.FindAll({
            param($node)
            $node -is [System.Management.Automation.Language.FunctionDefinitionAst] -and $node.Name -in $helpers
        }, $true)
    foreach ($function in $functions) { . ([scriptblock]::Create($function.Extent.Text)) }

    # A repository with the tool's sources, its build output, the installed copy and the stamp of its build, every file
    # written at $Time.
    function New-Repository([datetime]$Time) {
        $root = Join-Path $TestDrive ([guid]::NewGuid().ToString('N'))
        foreach ($file in 'tools\eval\build.gradle.kts', 'tools\eval\src\main\kotlin\dev\kestrel\eval\Main.kt',
            'tools\eval\src\test\kotlin\dev\kestrel\eval\CliTest.kt', 'tools\eval\build\install\eval\lib\eval.jar',
            'tools\eval\build\eval-built.stamp', 'tools\eval\build\classes\kotlin\main\Main.class',
            'build-logic\convention\src\main\kotlin\dev\kestrel\build\JvmLibraryConventionPlugin.kt',
            'build-logic\convention\build\libs\convention.jar', 'gradle\libs.versions.toml', 'settings.gradle.kts') {
            $path = Join-Path $root $file
            New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force -Path (Split-Path -Parent $path) | Out-Null
            [IO.File]::WriteAllText($path, 'x')
            (Get-Item -LiteralPath $path).LastWriteTimeUtc = $Time
        }
        $root
    }

    function Set-WriteTime([string]$Root, [string]$File, [datetime]$Time) { (Get-Item -LiteralPath (Join-Path $Root $File)).LastWriteTimeUtc = $Time }
}

Describe 'scripts\eval.ps1' {
    BeforeEach {
        $built = [datetime]::new(2026, 11, 23, 12, 0, 0, [DateTimeKind]::Utc)
        $later = $built.AddMinutes(5)
    }

    It 'parses, and finds its helpers' {
        $errors | Should -BeNullOrEmpty
        @($functions).Count | Should -Be $helpers.Count
    }

    It 'rebuilds when the installed copy or the stamp of its build is missing' {
        foreach ($file in 'tools\eval\build\install\eval\lib\eval.jar', 'tools\eval\build\eval-built.stamp') {
            $root = New-Repository $built
            Remove-Item -LiteralPath (Join-Path $root $file)

            Test-EvalStale $root | Should -BeTrue -Because $file
        }
    }

    It 'does not rebuild when nothing changed since the installed copy' {
        Test-EvalStale (New-Repository $built) | Should -BeFalse
    }

    It 'rebuilds when a source of the tool, the build logic, the version catalog or the settings is newer than the installed copy' {
        foreach ($file in 'tools\eval\src\main\kotlin\dev\kestrel\eval\Main.kt', 'tools\eval\build.gradle.kts',
            'build-logic\convention\src\main\kotlin\dev\kestrel\build\JvmLibraryConventionPlugin.kt', 'gradle\libs.versions.toml', 'settings.gradle.kts') {
            $root = New-Repository $built
            Set-WriteTime $root $file $later

            Test-EvalStale $root | Should -BeTrue -Because $file
        }
    }

    It 'does not rebuild for a newer file in the tool''s build folder or in build-logic''s build output' {
        $root = New-Repository $built
        Set-WriteTime $root 'tools\eval\build\classes\kotlin\main\Main.class' $later
        Set-WriteTime $root 'build-logic\convention\build\libs\convention.jar' $later

        Test-EvalStale $root | Should -BeFalse
    }

    It 'builds the installed copy only when it is out of date, and stops the Gradle daemon after the build, also after a failed one' {
        $calls = [System.Collections.Generic.List[string]]::new()
        Mock Invoke-Gradle { $calls.Add($Arguments -join ' '); 0 }

        Update-EvalTool (New-Repository $built) | Should -BeTrue
        $calls.Count | Should -Be 0

        $root = New-Repository $built
        Set-WriteTime $root 'tools\eval\build.gradle.kts' $later
        Update-EvalTool $root | Should -BeTrue
        @($calls) | Should -Be @(':tools:eval:installDist', '--stop')

        $calls.Clear()
        Mock Invoke-Gradle { $calls.Add($Arguments -join ' '); if ($Arguments -contains '--stop') { 0 } else { 1 } }
        $root = New-Repository $built
        Set-WriteTime $root 'tools\eval\build.gradle.kts' $later
        Update-EvalTool $root | Should -BeFalse
        @($calls) | Should -Be @(':tools:eval:installDist', '--stop')
        Test-EvalStale $root | Should -BeTrue
    }

    It 'is current after a build that left eval.jar as it was, because only a test of the tool changed' {
        # Times before now, because Update-EvalTool writes the stamp at the time of the build.
        $root = New-Repository ([datetime]::UtcNow.AddHours(-2))
        Set-WriteTime $root 'tools\eval\src\test\kotlin\dev\kestrel\eval\CliTest.kt' ([datetime]::UtcNow.AddHours(-1))
        $jar = (Get-Item -LiteralPath (Join-Path $root 'tools\eval\build\install\eval\lib\eval.jar')).LastWriteTimeUtc
        Mock Invoke-Gradle { 0 }
        Test-EvalStale $root | Should -BeTrue

        Update-EvalTool $root | Should -BeTrue

        (Get-Item -LiteralPath (Join-Path $root 'tools\eval\build\install\eval\lib\eval.jar')).LastWriteTimeUtc | Should -Be $jar
        Test-EvalStale $root | Should -BeFalse
    }
}
```

Create `scripts/eval.ps1`:

```powershell
# Runs :tools:eval, the agent's task suite on the PC (design spec, section 16.5), with this script's own arguments, and
# ends with the tool's exit code: 0 when every run passed or was skipped with a reason, 1 when a run failed, and 2 for a
# refusal before any run (a device, the live gate, a missing configuration, or a build of the tool that failed).
# It rebuilds the tool's installed copy (tools\eval\build\install\eval) with scripts\gradle.ps1 :tools:eval:installDist
# only when a file of tools\eval (outside its build folder), of build-logic (outside its build output), or
# gradle\libs.versions.toml or settings.gradle.kts is newer than the stamp tools\eval\build\eval-built.stamp, which it
# writes after each build that succeeded, and then stops the Gradle daemon, as AGENTS.md asks. The tool runs in the
# repository's folder, where it finds corpus\owner, eval-results and
# scripts\install.ps1. Ctrl+C reaches the tool, whose shutdown hook puts the device back; Windows then asks "Terminate
# batch job (Y/N)?", and either answer is safe. Commands: run, suite, injection, rehearse and list (scripts\eval.ps1
# without arguments prints them). In PowerShell, quote a list, as in --tasks 'stand-in-hotspot,stand-in-read', because
# PowerShell would pass a,b on as two arguments.
# Example: scripts\eval.ps1 run --device emulator-5554 --task stand-in-hotspot --dry-run
$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'
. "$PSScriptRoot\lib\Kestrel.ps1"

# The newest write time of the files whose change needs a new build of the tool: its own files outside its build folder,
# the build logic outside its build output, the version catalog and the settings. build-logic's sources hold a package
# folder named "build" (dev\kestrel\build), so only build-logic's own output folders are left out.
function Get-NewestSource([string]$Root) {
    $tool = Join-Path $Root 'tools\eval'
    $logic = Join-Path $Root 'build-logic'
    $files = @(Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $tool -Recurse -File | Where-Object { $_.FullName.Substring($tool.Length + 1) -notmatch '^(build|\.gradle|\.kotlin)\\' })
    $files += @(Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $logic -Recurse -File | Where-Object { $_.FullName.Substring($logic.Length + 1) -notmatch '^((convention\\)?build|\.gradle|\.kotlin)\\' })
    $files += @(Get-Item -LiteralPath (Join-Path $Root 'gradle\libs.versions.toml'), (Join-Path $Root 'settings.gradle.kts'))
    ($files | Measure-Object -Property LastWriteTimeUtc -Maximum).Maximum
}

# Whether the installed copy is missing or older than a source of the tool. The sources are compared with the stamp that
# Update-EvalTool writes after a build, not with eval.jar, because installDist leaves eval.jar as it was when a change (a
# test, or a file saved without a change) does not change the jar, and the copy would then be out of date for ever. The
# stamp is in the build folder, which Get-NewestSource leaves out, and outside install\eval, which installDist empties.
function Test-EvalStale([string]$Root) {
    $jar = Join-Path $Root 'tools\eval\build\install\eval\lib\eval.jar'
    $stamp = Join-Path $Root 'tools\eval\build\eval-built.stamp'
    if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $jar) -or -not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $stamp)) { return $true }
    (Get-NewestSource $Root) -gt (Get-Item -LiteralPath $stamp).LastWriteTimeUtc
}

# Runs scripts\gradle.ps1 with the arguments, its output on the console, and returns its exit code.
function Invoke-Gradle([string[]]$Arguments) {
    & "$PSScriptRoot\gradle.ps1" @Arguments | Out-Host
    $LASTEXITCODE
}

# Builds the installed copy when it is out of date, and stops the Gradle daemon afterwards, also after a failed build.
# After a build that succeeded, it writes the stamp that Test-EvalStale reads. Returns whether the installed copy is current.
function Update-EvalTool([string]$Root) {
    if (-not (Test-EvalStale $Root)) { return $true }
    $built = Invoke-Gradle @(':tools:eval:installDist')
    Invoke-Gradle @('--stop') | Out-Null
    if ($built -ne 0) { return $false }
    [IO.File]::WriteAllText((Join-Path $Root 'tools\eval\build\eval-built.stamp'), '')
    $true
}

$root = (Resolve-Path (Join-Path $PSScriptRoot '..')).Path
if (-not (Update-EvalTool $root)) {
    Write-Warning 'scripts\gradle.ps1 :tools:eval:installDist failed, so :tools:eval did not run.'
    exit 2
}
# The tool finds adb through ANDROID_HOME and runs on JAVA_HOME, which Use-KestrelToolchain sets.
Use-KestrelToolchain
Push-Location -LiteralPath $root
try {
    & (Join-Path $root 'tools\eval\build\install\eval\bin\eval.bat') @args
    $code = $LASTEXITCODE
} finally {
    Pop-Location
}
exit $code
```

```powershell
function Get-DeviceTaskArgument([string[]]$Arguments) {

}
```

with:

```powershell
# uninstalls, connected (on-device) tests and device checks. Options, which start with '-', are not tasks. installDist of
# Gradle's application plugin, which scripts\eval.ps1 runs to build :tools:eval's copy on this computer, reaches no device.
# Gradle also accepts abbreviated task names, which this check misses, so scripts\gradle.ps1 has a second guard.
function Get-DeviceTaskArgument([string[]]$Arguments) {
    @($Arguments | Where-Object { $_ -notlike '-*' -and ($_ -split ':')[-1] -match 'install|connected|devicecheck' -and ($_ -split ':')[-1] -ne 'installDist' })
}
```

### Task 2: `device`: adb, the guard, the guarded shell and the dump readers

**Files:**
- Create: `tools/eval/src/main/kotlin/dev/kestrel/eval/device/Adb.kt`, `ProcessAdb.kt`, `Clock.kt`, `DeviceGuard.kt`, `Device.kt`, `Dumps.kt`
- Test: `tools/eval/src/test/kotlin/dev/kestrel/eval/device/FakeAdb.kt`, `FakeClock.kt`, `DeviceGuardTest.kt`, `DeviceTest.kt`, `DumpsTest.kt`, `ProcessAdbTest.kt`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 1's module.
- Produces, in `dev.kestrel.eval.device`:
  - `data class ShellResult(val exitCode: Int, val out: String, val err: String)`; `class AdbException(message: String) : Exception(message)`; `interface Adb { val serial: String; fun shell(command: String, timeout: Duration): ShellResult; fun host(args: List<String>, timeout: Duration): ShellResult }`.
  - `fun interface Processes { fun run(command: List<String>, timeout: Duration): ShellResult }`, `object LocalProcesses : Processes`, `class ProcessAdb(program: String, serial: String, processes: Processes = LocalProcesses) : Adb`, which refuses a command with a double quote.
  - `interface Clock { fun elapsed(): Duration; fun sleep(duration: Duration); fun now(): LocalDateTime }`, `object SystemClock : Clock`.
  - `enum class DeviceKind(val label: String, val folder: String) { EMULATOR("kestrel-api37", "kestrel-api37"), A71("the A71", "a71") }`, `data class TestDevice(val serial: String, val kind: DeviceKind, val sdk: Int)`, `class Refusal(message: String) : Exception(message)`, `class DeviceGuard(clock: Clock = SystemClock) { fun accept(adb: Adb): TestDevice }` with `DeviceGuard.EMULATOR_AVD`, `A71_SERIAL`, `A71_MODEL`, `DAILY_PHONE` and `fun consoleName(adb: Adb): String`.
  - `class DeviceCommandFailed(message: String) : Exception(message)`, `class DeviceChanged(message: String) : Exception(message)`, `class Device(adb: Adb, val test: TestDevice, clock: Clock = SystemClock)` with `val serial: String`, `val kind: DeviceKind`, `fun read(command: String, timeout: Duration = Device.TIMEOUT): List<String>`, `fun idempotent(command: String, timeout: Duration = Device.TIMEOUT): List<String>`, `fun once(command: String, timeout: Duration = Device.TIMEOUT): List<String>?` (null when the answer was lost), `fun emu(args: List<String>, timeout: Duration = Device.TIMEOUT): String`, `fun push(local: Path, remote: String)` and `fun guarded(command: String): String`.
  - `object Dumps` with `wazeNavigating(dump: List<String>): Boolean`, `hotspotOn(dump): Boolean`, `wifiValidated(dump): Boolean`, `pressKinds(lines): List<String>`, `treeImageCount(json: String): Int`, `resumedPackage(dump): String?`, `grantedPermissions(dump, permissions: List<String>): List<String>`, `largestRowId(rows): Long`, `rowCount(rows): Int`, `callActive(registry): Boolean`, `accountCount(dump, type: String): Int`, `packageListed(list, packageName: String): Boolean` and `appOpMode(lines, op: String): String` ("default" without an entry).
  - Test fixtures, which every later task's tests use: `class FakeAdb(serial: String = "emulator-5554", model: String = "sdk_gphone64_x86_64", avd: String = "kestrel-api37", sdk: Int = 37) : Adb` with `props`, `consoleName`, `state`, `commands` (the commands inside the guard), `raw`, `hostCommands`, `settings`, `files`, `fun on(pattern: String, reply: (MatchResult) -> String?)` (null for a failed command; the newest rule wins), `fun hook(action: String, answer: (Map<String, String>) -> String)`, `fun hookFails(action: String, reason: String)`, `fun loseNext(pattern: String)` and `fun hookCalls(action: String): List<Map<String, String>>`; `class FakeClock(var time: Duration = Duration.ZERO) : Clock` with `sleeps` and `onSleep`, whose `now()` starts at 2026-11-23 12:00:00.

Design D3's guard and retry rule, and D5's dump readers. A guarded command is `Format-TestDeviceCommand`'s: `case $(getprop <property>) in <pattern>) <command> && echo && echo kestrel-end;; *) echo kestrel-refused;; esac`. A missing `kestrel-end` is a lost answer (adb failed, or the command failed on the device): `read` and `idempotent` try again up to 3 times, 2 s apart, and `once` answers null, so that the step reads the state back instead of sending the command again. `kestrel-refused` is another device on the serial, and stops everything (`DeviceChanged`). The readers are task-check.ps1's, `Kestrel.ps1`'s, `Oracle.ps1`'s and a71-agent.ps1's, tested with the dumps of their Pester tests.

Counts: `:tools:eval` JVM N + 22 (`DeviceGuardTest` 6, `DeviceTest` 8, `DumpsTest` 5, `ProcessAdbTest` 3).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the fakes and the failing tests**

Create `tools/eval/src/test/kotlin/dev/kestrel/eval/device/FakeAdb.kt`:

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.eval.device

import kotlin.time.Duration

/**
 * A small device for the tests (design D3 of sub-project 6): properties, settings, files, the answers of commands and of
 * the debug hooks by action, and every command that reached it. A guarded command runs only when the guard's property
 * matches its pattern, as on a real device; [loseNext] loses the next answer of a matching command, as adb does when a
 * device goes away for a moment, and [hookCalls] gives the extras of each broadcast.
 */
class FakeAdb(
    override val serial: String = "emulator-5554",
    model: String = "sdk_gphone64_x86_64",
    avd: String = "kestrel-api37",
    sdk: Int = 37,
) : Adb {
    val props = mutableMapOf("ro.product.model" to model, "ro.boot.qemu.avd_name" to avd, "ro.build.version.sdk" to "$sdk")
    var consoleName = avd
    var state = "device"

    /** The commands inside the guard, in their order. */
    val commands = mutableListOf<String>()

    /** The guarded commands as they went to adb. */
    val raw = mutableListOf<String>()
    val hostCommands = mutableListOf<List<String>>()
    val settings = mutableMapOf<String, String>()
    val files = mutableMapOf<String, String>()
    private val handlers = mutableListOf<Pair<Regex, (MatchResult) -> String?>>()
    private val hooks = mutableMapOf<String, (Map<String, String>) -> Pair<Int, String>>()
    private val broadcasts = mutableListOf<Pair<String, Map<String, String>>>()
    private val lost = mutableListOf<Regex>()

    /** Answers a command that matches [pattern] (the newest rule wins) with its output, or null for a failed command. */
    fun on(pattern: String, reply: (MatchResult) -> String?) {
        handlers.add(0, Regex(pattern) to reply)
    }

    /** Answers the debug hook [action] (such as AGENT_TASK) with `name=value` pairs, as result -1. */
    fun hook(action: String, answer: (Map<String, String>) -> String) {
        hooks[action] = { extras -> -1 to answer(extras) }
    }

    /** Answers the debug hook [action] with an error code and its reason. */
    fun hookFails(action: String, reason: String) {
        hooks[action] = { _ -> 1 to reason }
    }

    /** Loses the next answer of a command that matches [pattern]: the command reached the device, but adb gave no answer. */
    fun loseNext(pattern: String) {
        lost.add(Regex(pattern))
    }

    /** The extras of each broadcast of [action], in their order. */
    fun hookCalls(action: String): List<Map<String, String>> = broadcasts.filter { it.first == action }.map { it.second }

    override fun shell(command: String, timeout: Duration): ShellResult {
        val guard = GUARD.matchEntire(command)
        if (guard == null) {
            commands.add(command)
            return ShellResult(0, answer(command).orEmpty() + "\n", "")
        }
        raw.add(command)
        val (property, pattern, inner) = guard.destructured
        val expected = Regex(Regex.escape(pattern).replace("*", "\\E.*\\Q"))
        if (!expected.matches(props[property].orEmpty())) return ShellResult(0, "kestrel-refused\n", "")
        commands.add(inner)
        val loss = lost.firstOrNull { it.containsMatchIn(inner) }
        if (loss != null) {
            lost.remove(loss)
            // The command ran on the device; only its answer is lost.
            answer(inner)
            return ShellResult(255, "", "error: closed")
        }
        val output = answer(inner) ?: return ShellResult(1, "", "the command failed")
        val text = if (output.isEmpty() || output.endsWith("\n")) output else "$output\n"
        return ShellResult(0, "$text\nkestrel-end\n", "")
    }

    override fun host(args: List<String>, timeout: Duration): ShellResult {
        hostCommands.add(args)
        return when {
            args == listOf("get-state") -> ShellResult(0, "$state\n", "")
            args == listOf("emu", "avd", "name") -> ShellResult(0, "$consoleName\r\nOK\r\n", "")
            else -> ShellResult(0, "", "")
        }
    }

    private fun answer(command: String): String? {
        for ((regex, reply) in handlers) {
            val match = regex.find(command) ?: continue
            return reply(match)
        }
        GETPROP.matchEntire(command)?.let { return props[it.groupValues[1]].orEmpty() }
        SETTINGS_GET.matchEntire(command)?.let { return settings["${it.groupValues[1]}/${it.groupValues[2]}"] ?: "null" }
        SETTINGS_PUT.matchEntire(command)?.let {
            settings["${it.groupValues[1]}/${it.groupValues[2]}"] = it.groupValues[3].removeSurrounding("'")
            return ""
        }
        RUN_AS_CAT.matchEntire(command)?.let { return files[it.groupValues[1]] }
        BROADCAST.matchEntire(command)?.let { return broadcast(it.groupValues[1], it.groupValues[2]) }
        return ""
    }

    private fun broadcast(action: String, extras: String): String {
        val parsed = parseExtras(extras)
        broadcasts.add(action to parsed)
        val reply = hooks[action] ?: return "Broadcasting: Intent { act=dev.kestrel.hook.$action }\nBroadcast completed: result=0"
        val (code, data) = reply(parsed)
        return "Broadcasting: Intent { act=dev.kestrel.hook.$action }\nBroadcast completed: result=$code, data=\"$data\""
    }

    companion object {
        private val GUARD = Regex("case \\$\\(getprop (\\S+)\\) in (\\S+)\\) (.*) && echo && echo kestrel-end;; \\*\\) echo kestrel-refused;; esac", RegexOption.DOT_MATCHES_ALL)
        private val GETPROP = Regex("getprop (\\S+)")
        private val SETTINGS_GET = Regex("settings get (\\S+) (\\S+)")
        private val SETTINGS_PUT = Regex("settings put (\\S+) (\\S+) (.+)")
        private val RUN_AS_CAT = Regex("run-as dev\\.kestrel cat (\\S+)")
        private val BROADCAST = Regex("am broadcast -n dev\\.kestrel/dev\\.kestrel\\.app\\.debug\\.TestHookReceiver -a dev\\.kestrel\\.hook\\.(\\S+)(.*)", RegexOption.DOT_MATCHES_ALL)

        /** The extras of `--es name 'text'`, `--ez name true` and `--ei name 5`, with the shell's quoting undone. */
        fun parseExtras(text: String): Map<String, String> {
            val extras = linkedMapOf<String, String>()
            var at = 0
            fun skipSpaces() {
                while (at < text.length && text[at] == ' ') at++
            }
            fun word(): String {
                skipSpaces()
                val builder = StringBuilder()
                while (at < text.length && text[at] != ' ') {
                    if (text[at] == '\'') {
                        at++
                        while (text[at] != '\'') builder.append(text[at++])
                        at++
                        // '\'' is a quote that the shell's quoting closed, escaped and opened again.
                        if (text.startsWith("\\''", at)) {
                            builder.append('\'')
                            at += 2
                        }
                    } else {
                        builder.append(text[at++])
                    }
                }
                return builder.toString()
            }
            while (true) {
                skipSpaces()
                if (at >= text.length) break
                word()
                val name = word()
                extras[name] = word()
            }
            return extras
        }
    }
}
```

Create `tools/eval/src/test/kotlin/dev/kestrel/eval/device/FakeClock.kt`:

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.eval.device

import java.time.LocalDateTime
import kotlin.time.Duration

/** A clock for the tests: it never waits, and each sleep moves its time on and runs [onSleep]. */
class FakeClock(var time: Duration = Duration.ZERO) : Clock {
    val sleeps = mutableListOf<Duration>()
    var onSleep: (Duration) -> Unit = {}

    override fun elapsed(): Duration = time

    override fun sleep(duration: Duration) {
        sleeps.add(duration)
        time += duration
        onSleep(duration)
    }

    override fun now(): LocalDateTime = START.plusNanos(time.inWholeNanoseconds)

    companion object {
        val START: LocalDateTime = LocalDateTime.of(2026, 11, 23, 12, 0, 0)
    }
}
```

Create `tools/eval/src/test/kotlin/dev/kestrel/eval/device/DeviceGuardTest.kt`:

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.eval.device

import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
import org.junit.Assert.assertThrows
import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
import org.junit.Test
import kotlin.time.Duration.Companion.seconds

/** Only kestrel-api37 and the A71, never the S23+ (design D3 of sub-project 6). */
class DeviceGuardTest {
    private val clock = FakeClock()
    private val guard = DeviceGuard(clock)

    @Test
    fun acceptsKestrelApi37ByItsAvdNameAndItsConsole() {
        assertEquals(TestDevice("emulator-5554", DeviceKind.EMULATOR, 37), guard.accept(FakeAdb()))
    }

    @Test
    fun refusesAnEmulatorWhoseConsoleNamesAnotherAvd() {
        val adb = FakeAdb().apply { consoleName = "zoran" }

        val refusal = assertThrows(Refusal::class.java) { guard.accept(adb) }

        assertEquals("Refusing emulator-5554 (model 'sdk_gphone64_x86_64', AVD 'kestrel-api37'). :tools:eval runs only on kestrel-api37 and the Galaxy A71.", refusal.message)
        // A busy emulator sometimes answers nothing, so a refusal is checked twice more, 2 s apart.
        assertEquals(listOf(2.seconds, 2.seconds), clock.sleeps)
    }

    @Test
    fun acceptsTheA71ByItsSerialAndItsModel() {
        val adb = FakeAdb(serial = "R58N66RVD7Z", model = "SM-A715F", avd = "", sdk = 33)

        assertEquals(TestDevice("R58N66RVD7Z", DeviceKind.A71, 33), guard.accept(adb))
    }

    @Test
    fun refusesAnA71ModelWithAnotherSerial() {
        val adb = FakeAdb(serial = "R58N00000000", model = "SM-A715F", avd = "", sdk = 33)

        assertThrows(Refusal::class.java) { guard.accept(adb) }
    }

    @Test
    fun refusesTheS23PlusAtOnceWithItsOwnMessage() {
        val adb = FakeAdb(serial = "R5CW00000000", model = "SM-S916B", avd = "", sdk = 36)

        val refusal = assertThrows(Refusal::class.java) { guard.accept(adb) }

        assertEquals("Refusing R5CW00000000: it is the owner's Galaxy S23+ (SM-S916B), which :tools:eval never uses.", refusal.message)
        assertTrue(clock.sleeps.isEmpty())
    }

    @Test
    fun refusesADeviceThatIsNotConnected() {
        val adb = FakeAdb().apply { state = "offline" }

        val refusal = assertThrows(Refusal::class.java) { guard.accept(adb) }

        assertEquals("Device emulator-5554 is not connected.", refusal.message)
    }
}
```

Create `tools/eval/src/test/kotlin/dev/kestrel/eval/device/DeviceTest.kt`:

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.eval.device

import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
import org.junit.Assert.assertNull
import org.junit.Assert.assertThrows
import org.junit.Test
import java.nio.file.Paths
import kotlin.time.Duration.Companion.seconds

/** The guarded device and its retry rule: reads are tried 3 times, a command that acts runs once (design D3 of sub-project 6). */
class DeviceTest {
    private val clock = FakeClock()
    private val adb = FakeAdb()
    private val device = Device(adb, TestDevice("emulator-5554", DeviceKind.EMULATOR, 37), clock)

    @Test
    fun everyCommandRunsInsideTheGuardsCheckInTheSameShellCall() {
        adb.on("^dumpsys battery$") { "  level: 100" }

        assertEquals(listOf("  level: 100"), device.read("dumpsys battery"))
        assertEquals(
            "case \$(getprop ro.boot.qemu.avd_name) in kestrel-api37) dumpsys battery && echo && echo kestrel-end;; *) echo kestrel-refused;; esac",
            adb.raw.single(),
        )
        val a71 = Device(FakeAdb(serial = "R58N66RVD7Z", model = "SM-A715F", avd = ""), TestDevice("R58N66RVD7Z", DeviceKind.A71, 33), clock)
        assertEquals(
            "case \$(getprop ro.product.model) in SM-A715F*) true && echo && echo kestrel-end;; *) echo kestrel-refused;; esac",
            a71.guarded("true"),
        )
    }

    @Test
    fun anEmptyAnswerIsNoFailure() {
        assertEquals(emptyList<String>(), device.idempotent("settings put global zen_mode 0"))
    }

    @Test
    fun aReadIsTriedAgainAfterALostAnswer() {
        adb.on("^getprop ro.build.version.sdk$") { "37" }
        adb.loseNext("^getprop")

        assertEquals(listOf("37"), device.read("getprop ro.build.version.sdk"))
        assertEquals(2, adb.commands.size)
        assertEquals(listOf(2.seconds), clock.sleeps)
    }

    @Test
    fun aReadFailsAfterThreeLostAnswers() {
        repeat(3) { adb.loseNext("^dumpsys") }

        val failure = assertThrows(DeviceCommandFailed::class.java) { device.read("dumpsys tethering") }

        assertEquals("Could not run 'dumpsys tethering' on emulator-5554: error: closed", failure.message)
        assertEquals(3, adb.commands.size)
    }

    @Test
    fun aCommandThatActsIsNeverSentTwice() {
        adb.loseNext("^input keyevent")

        assertNull(device.once("input keyevent KEYCODE_ENDCALL"))
        assertEquals(listOf("input keyevent KEYCODE_ENDCALL"), adb.commands)
        assertEquals(emptyList<kotlin.time.Duration>(), clock.sleeps)
        assertEquals(emptyList<String>(), device.once("input keyevent KEYCODE_ENDCALL"))
    }

    @Test
    fun anotherDeviceThatTookTheSerialStopsEveryCommand() {
        adb.props["ro.boot.qemu.avd_name"] = "zoran"

        val changed = assertThrows(DeviceChanged::class.java) { device.read("dumpsys battery") }

        assertEquals("Refusing to run 'dumpsys battery' on emulator-5554: it is no longer the test device that :tools:eval accepted.", changed.message)
        assertEquals(emptyList<String>(), adb.commands)
    }

    @Test
    fun theConsoleIsCheckedRightBeforeEachEmuCommand() {
        device.emu(listOf("sms", "send", "+12025550177", "maple"))
        adb.consoleName = "zoran"

        assertThrows(DeviceChanged::class.java) { device.emu(listOf("sms", "send", "+12025550177", "heron")) }
        assertEquals(
            listOf(listOf("emu", "avd", "name"), listOf("emu", "sms", "send", "+12025550177", "maple"), listOf("emu", "avd", "name")),
            adb.hostCommands,
        )
    }

    @Test
    fun aPushChecksTheDeviceFirst() {
        val file = Paths.get("build", "task.json").toAbsolutePath()

        device.push(file, "/data/local/tmp/kestrel-task.json")

        assertEquals(listOf("true"), adb.commands)
        assertEquals(listOf(listOf("push", file.toString(), "/data/local/tmp/kestrel-task.json")), adb.hostCommands)
    }
}
```

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.eval.device

import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
import org.junit.Assert.assertFalse
import org.junit.Assert.assertNull
import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue

/** The readers of Android's dumps, with the dumps of the Pester tests that tested them in PowerShell (design D5 of sub-project 6). */
class DumpsTest {
    @Test
    fun wazeNavigatesWhenItsNotificationIsOngoing() {
        val dump = listOf(
            "  NotificationRecord(0x0a pkg=com.example.chat user=UserHandle{0} id=1 tag=null importance=3)",
            "    flags=0x2",
            "  NotificationRecord(0x0b pkg=com.waze user=UserHandle{0} id=7 tag=null importance=2)",
            "    flags=0x62",
        )

        assertTrue(Dumps.wazeNavigating(dump))
        assertFalse(Dumps.wazeNavigating(listOf("  NotificationRecord(0x0b pkg=com.waze user=UserHandle{0} id=7)", "    flags=0x10")))
    }

    @Test
    fun theHotspotAndAValidatedWifi() {
        assertTrue(Dumps.hotspotOn(listOf("Tethering:", "  wlan1 - TetheredState")))
        assertFalse(Dumps.hotspotOn(listOf("Tethering:", "  wlan1 - AvailableState")))
        assertTrue(Dumps.wifiValidated(listOf("  Capabilities: INTERNET&NOT_RESTRICTED&VALIDATED Transports: WIFI")))
        assertFalse(Dumps.wifiValidated(listOf("  Capabilities: INTERNET&NOT_RESTRICTED Transports: WIFI")))
    }

    @Test
    fun thePressesAndTheImagesOfTheTree() {
        assertEquals(listOf("send", "share"), Dumps.pressKinds(listOf("send 1795086000000", "", "share 1795086001000", "nonsense")))
        val json = """{"windows":[{"id":3,"kind":"APPLICATION","active":true},{"id":4,"kind":"SYSTEM","active":false}],"elements":[""" +
            """{"windowId":3,"className":"android.widget.ImageView"},{"windowId":3,"className":"android.widget.ImageView"},""" +
            """{"windowId":3,"className":"android.widget.TextView"},{"windowId":4,"className":"android.widget.ImageView"}]}"""
        assertEquals(2, Dumps.treeImageCount(json))
        assertEquals(0, Dumps.treeImageCount("""{"windows":[],"elements":[]}"""))
    }

    @Test
    fun thePackageInFront() {
        val activities = listOf(
            "Display #0 (activities from top to bottom):",
            "  * Task{6c1 #12 type=standard A=1000:com.android.settings}",
            "  topResumedActivity=ActivityRecord{9f3e2b1 u0 com.android.settings/.Settings t12}",
        )

        assertEquals("com.android.settings", Dumps.resumedPackage(activities))
        assertEquals(
            "com.sec.android.app.launcher",
            Dumps.resumedPackage(listOf("  mResumedActivity: ActivityRecord{1 u0 com.sec.android.app.launcher/.activities.LauncherActivity t1}")),
        )
        assertNull(Dumps.resumedPackage(listOf("  topResumedActivity=null")))
    }

    @Test

            "        android.permission.READ_CONTACTS: granted=false, flags=[ ]",
        )
        assertEquals(
            listOf("android.permission.RECORD_AUDIO"),
            Dumps.grantedPermissions(dump, listOf("android.permission.RECORD_AUDIO", "android.permission.READ_CONTACTS")),
        )
        assertEquals(12L, Dumps.largestRowId(listOf("Row: 0 _id=3", "Row: 1 _id=12", "Row: 2 _id=7")))
        assertEquals(0L, Dumps.largestRowId(listOf("No result found.")))
        assertEquals(0, Dumps.rowCount(listOf("No result found.")))
        assertTrue(Dumps.callActive(listOf("  mCallState=0", "  mCallState=2")))
        assertFalse(Dumps.callActive(listOf("  mCallState=0")))
        val accounts = listOf("Accounts: 2", "    Account {name=pera@example.com, type=com.google}", "    Account {name=Pera, type=org.telegram.messenger}")
        assertEquals(1, Dumps.accountCount(accounts, "com.google"))
        assertTrue(Dumps.packageListed(listOf("package:dev.kestrel.testapp", "package:dev.kestrel"), "dev.kestrel"))
        assertFalse(Dumps.packageListed(listOf("package:dev.kestrel.testapp"), "dev.kestrel"))
        assertEquals("allow", Dumps.appOpMode(listOf("SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW: allow; time=+1m2s ago"), "SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW"))
        assertEquals("default", Dumps.appOpMode(listOf("No operations.", "Default mode: allow"), "SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW"))
        assertEquals("default", Dumps.appOpMode(listOf("Uid mode: SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW: ignore"), "SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW"))
    }
```

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.eval.device

import org.junit.Assert.assertEquals
import org.junit.Assert.assertThrows
import org.junit.Assert.assertTrue
import org.junit.Test
import kotlin.time.Duration.Companion.seconds

/** adb's command lines, and a program that runs past its time (design D3 of sub-project 6). */
class ProcessAdbTest {
    @Test
    fun shellAndHostCommandsGoToAdbWithTheSerial() {
        val ran = mutableListOf<List<String>>()
        val adb = ProcessAdb("adb.exe", "emulator-5554") { command, _ -> ran.add(command); ShellResult(0, "", "") }

        adb.shell("dumpsys battery", 30.seconds)
        adb.host(listOf("emu", "avd", "name"), 30.seconds)

        assertEquals(listOf(listOf("adb.exe", "-s", "emulator-5554", "shell", "dumpsys battery"), listOf("adb.exe", "-s", "emulator-5554", "emu", "avd", "name")), ran)
        assertThrows(IllegalArgumentException::class.java) { adb.shell("echo \"x\"", 30.seconds) }
    }

    @Test
    fun aProgramRunsAndAnswers() {
        val result = LocalProcesses.run(listOf(java(), "-version"), 60.seconds)

        assertEquals(0, result.exitCode)
        assertTrue(result.err.contains("version"))
    }

    @Test
    fun aProgramThatRunsPastItsTimeIsEnded() {
        val command = listOf(java(), "-cp", System.getProperty("java.class.path"), SleepMain::class.java.name)

        val failure = assertThrows(AdbException::class.java) { LocalProcesses.run(command, 2.seconds) }

        assertTrue(failure.message!!.contains("did not end within 2s"))
    }

    private fun java(): String = ProcessHandle.current().info().command().get()
}

/** A program that only waits, for the test of the timeout. */
object SleepMain {
    @JvmStatic
    fun main(args: Array<String>) {
        Thread.sleep(60_000)
    }
}
```

Create `tools/eval/src/main/kotlin/dev/kestrel/eval/device/Adb.kt`:

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.eval.device

import kotlin.time.Duration

/** What adb answered: the exit code that it passed on, and its output and error output, read as UTF-8. */
data class ShellResult(val exitCode: Int, val out: String, val err: String)

/** adb could not start, or did not end within its time, so its answer is lost. */
class AdbException(message: String) : Exception(message)

/**
 * adb for one serial (design D3 of sub-project 6). [shell] runs `adb -s <serial> shell <command>` and [host] runs
 * `adb -s <serial> <args>`, such as `emu avd name` or `push`. Both throw [AdbException] when adb did not answer in time.
 * Only [Device] and [DeviceGuard] call it, so that every command on a device goes through the guard.
 */
interface Adb {
    val serial: String

    fun shell(command: String, timeout: Duration): ShellResult

    fun host(args: List<String>, timeout: Duration): ShellResult
}
```

Create `tools/eval/src/main/kotlin/dev/kestrel/eval/device/ProcessAdb.kt`:

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.eval.device

import java.io.ByteArrayOutputStream
import java.io.File
import java.io.IOException
import java.util.concurrent.TimeUnit
import kotlin.concurrent.thread
import kotlin.time.Duration

/** Runs a program on this computer and reads its output as UTF-8. A seam, so that the tests give their own. */
fun interface Processes {
    /** Runs [command], whose first item is the program; throws [AdbException] when it cannot start or runs past [timeout]. */
    fun run(command: List<String>, timeout: Duration): ShellResult
}

/** [Processes] through [ProcessBuilder]. A program that runs past its time is ended with its whole process tree. */
object LocalProcesses : Processes {
    private const val READER_MILLIS = 5_000L

    override fun run(command: List<String>, timeout: Duration): ShellResult {
        val program = File(command.first()).name
        val process = try {
            ProcessBuilder(command).start()
        } catch (e: IOException) {
            throw AdbException("$program could not start (${e.javaClass.simpleName}).")
        }
        process.outputStream.close()
        val out = ByteArrayOutputStream()
        val err = ByteArrayOutputStream()
        val readers = listOf(
            thread(isDaemon = true) { process.inputStream.use { it.copyTo(out) } },
            thread(isDaemon = true) { process.errorStream.use { it.copyTo(err) } },
        )
        if (!process.waitFor(timeout.inWholeMilliseconds, TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS)) {
            process.descendants().forEach { it.destroyForcibly() }
            process.destroyForcibly()
            throw AdbException("$program did not end within $timeout.")
        }
        readers.forEach { it.join(READER_MILLIS) }
        return ShellResult(process.exitValue(), out.toString(Charsets.UTF_8), err.toString(Charsets.UTF_8))
    }
}

/** [Adb] through the adb program of the Android SDK. */
class ProcessAdb(
    private val program: String,
    override val serial: String,
    private val processes: Processes = LocalProcesses,
) : Adb {
    override fun shell(command: String, timeout: Duration): ShellResult {
        // Windows hands a double quote inside an argument to adb in a form that the device's shell reads differently, so
        // every device command quotes with single quotes only, as the commands of scripts\lib\Kestrel.ps1 do.
        require('"' !in command) { "A device command may not hold a double quote." }
        return processes.run(listOf(program, "-s", serial, "shell", command), timeout)
    }

    override fun host(args: List<String>, timeout: Duration): ShellResult = processes.run(listOf(program, "-s", serial) + args, timeout)
}
```

Create `tools/eval/src/main/kotlin/dev/kestrel/eval/device/Clock.kt`:

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.eval.device

import java.time.LocalDateTime
import kotlin.time.Duration
import kotlin.time.Duration.Companion.nanoseconds

/** Time for the waits and the polls, and the local time for the results; the tests give a clock that only counts. */
interface Clock {
    /** The time since an arbitrary start, which only grows. */
    fun elapsed(): Duration

    fun sleep(duration: Duration)

    /** This computer's local date and time, for the results files. */
    fun now(): LocalDateTime
}

object SystemClock : Clock {
    private val start = System.nanoTime()

    override fun elapsed(): Duration = (System.nanoTime() - start).nanoseconds

    override fun sleep(duration: Duration) = Thread.sleep(duration.inWholeMilliseconds)

    override fun now(): LocalDateTime = LocalDateTime.now()
}
```

Create `tools/eval/src/main/kotlin/dev/kestrel/eval/device/DeviceGuard.kt`:

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.eval.device

import kotlin.time.Duration.Companion.seconds

/** The two test devices (design D1 of sub-project 6), with the name that the results use and the results folder's prefix. */
enum class DeviceKind(val label: String, val folder: String) {
    EMULATOR("kestrel-api37", "kestrel-api37"),
    A71("the A71", "a71"),
}

/** A device that [DeviceGuard] accepted, with its Android API level. */
data class TestDevice(val serial: String, val kind: DeviceKind, val sdk: Int)

/** A refusal before any run (exit code 2, design D2 of sub-project 6): a device, the live gate or a missing configuration. */
class Refusal(message: String) : Exception(message)

/**
 * Accepts only kestrel-api37, when `ro.boot.qemu.avd_name` and the console's `emu avd name` both say so, and the Galaxy
 * A71, by its serial and its model (design D3 of sub-project 6). The Galaxy S23+ is the owner's daily phone: it is
 * refused with its own message, and no option allows it. Every other device is refused too. A busy emulator sometimes
 * answers a query with nothing, so a refusal is checked twice more, 2 s apart, as Assert-TestDevice does.
 */
class DeviceGuard(private val clock: Clock = SystemClock) {
    fun accept(adb: Adb): TestDevice {
        var state = ""
        var model = ""
        var avd = ""
        repeat(TRIES) { attempt ->
            if (attempt > 0) clock.sleep(RETRY)
            state = answer { adb.host(listOf("get-state"), QUICK) }
            if (state != "device") return@repeat
            model = answer { adb.shell("getprop ro.product.model", QUICK) }
            avd = answer { adb.shell("getprop ro.boot.qemu.avd_name", QUICK) }
            if (model.startsWith(DAILY_PHONE)) {
                throw Refusal("Refusing ${adb.serial}: it is the owner's Galaxy S23+ ($model), which :tools:eval never uses.")
            }
            val kind = when {
                avd == EMULATOR_AVD && consoleName(adb) == EMULATOR_AVD -> DeviceKind.EMULATOR
                adb.serial == A71_SERIAL && model.startsWith(A71_MODEL) -> DeviceKind.A71
                else -> null
            }
            if (kind != null) {
                val sdk = answer { adb.shell("getprop ro.build.version.sdk", QUICK) }.toIntOrNull()
                    ?: throw Refusal("${adb.serial} did not say its Android version.")
                return TestDevice(adb.serial, kind, sdk)
            }
        }
        if (state != "device") throw Refusal("Device ${adb.serial} is not connected.")
        throw Refusal("Refusing ${adb.serial} (model '$model', AVD '$avd'). :tools:eval runs only on kestrel-api37 and the Galaxy A71.")
    }

    private fun answer(call: () -> ShellResult): String = try {
        call().out.trim()
    } catch (e: AdbException) {
        ""
    }

    companion object {
        const val EMULATOR_AVD = "kestrel-api37"
        const val A71_SERIAL = "R58N66RVD7Z"
        const val A71_MODEL = "SM-A715F"
        const val DAILY_PHONE = "SM-S916"
        private const val TRIES = 3
        private val RETRY = 2.seconds
        private val QUICK = 30.seconds

        /** The AVD name that the emulator's console gives, from the first line of `emu avd name`, or "" without one. */
        fun consoleName(adb: Adb): String = try {
            adb.host(listOf("emu", "avd", "name"), QUICK).out.lineSequence().firstOrNull()?.trim().orEmpty()
        } catch (e: AdbException) {
            ""
        }
    }
}
```

Create `tools/eval/src/main/kotlin/dev/kestrel/eval/device/Device.kt`:

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.eval.device

import java.nio.file.Path
import kotlin.time.Duration
import kotlin.time.Duration.Companion.minutes
import kotlin.time.Duration.Companion.seconds

/** A command on the device that failed three times, with the device's last complaint. */
class DeviceCommandFailed(message: String) : Exception(message)

/** The serial no longer names the device that [DeviceGuard] accepted, so nothing more runs on it. */
class DeviceChanged(message: String) : Exception(message)

/**
 * The device that [DeviceGuard] accepted (design D3 of sub-project 6). Every shell command runs inside the check of
 * `Format-TestDeviceCommand`, in the same `adb shell` call: `case $(getprop <property>) in <pattern>) <command> && echo
 * && echo kestrel-end;; *) echo kestrel-refused;; esac`, so that another device that took the serial is never changed,
 * and the marker tells an empty answer from a failed command. Each step chooses how its command runs:
 * - [read] and [idempotent] try up to 3 times, 2 s apart, as `Invoke-SettingsCommand` does, and are only for commands
 *   that do the same when they run twice;
 * - [once] runs a command that creates something or acts once, and answers null when its answer was lost; the step then
 *   reads the state back instead of sending the command again.
 */
class Device(private val adb: Adb, val test: TestDevice, private val clock: Clock = SystemClock) {
    val serial: String get() = test.serial
    val kind: DeviceKind get() = test.kind

    private val property = if (test.kind == DeviceKind.EMULATOR) "ro.boot.qemu.avd_name" else "ro.product.model"
    private val pattern = if (test.kind == DeviceKind.EMULATOR) DeviceGuard.EMULATOR_AVD else "${DeviceGuard.A71_MODEL}*"

    /** A command that only reads, such as `getprop`, `settings get`, `dumpsys` or `content query`. */
    fun read(command: String, timeout: Duration = TIMEOUT): List<String> = repeated(command, timeout)

    /**
     * A command that does the same when it runs twice: `settings put`, `cmd uimode night`, `cmd notification set_dnd`,
     * `svc`, `am force-stop`, `pm grant`, `pm revoke`, `appops set` and `rm -f`.
     */
    fun idempotent(command: String, timeout: Duration = TIMEOUT): List<String> = repeated(command, timeout)

    /** A command that runs once; null when its answer was lost, also when it failed on the device. */
    fun once(command: String, timeout: Duration = TIMEOUT): List<String>? = when (val answer = attempt(command, timeout)) {
        is Answer.Done -> answer.lines
        is Answer.Lost -> null
    }

    /** A command of the emulator's console, right after the check that the console still belongs to kestrel-api37. */
    fun emu(args: List<String>, timeout: Duration = TIMEOUT): String {
        if (kind != DeviceKind.EMULATOR) throw DeviceChanged("Only kestrel-api37 has an emulator console; $serial is ${kind.label}.")
        if (DeviceGuard.consoleName(adb) != DeviceGuard.EMULATOR_AVD) throw DeviceChanged(changed("emu ${args.joinToString(" ")}"))
        return adb.host(listOf("emu") + args, timeout).out
    }

    /** Copies a file of this computer to the device, after the guard's check; a copy overwrites, so it may run twice. */
    fun push(local: Path, remote: String) {
        read("true")
        var reason = "no answer"
        repeat(TRIES) { attempt ->
            if (attempt > 0) clock.sleep(RETRY)
            val result = try {
                adb.host(listOf("push", local.toString(), remote), PUSH_TIMEOUT)
            } catch (e: AdbException) {
                reason = e.message.orEmpty()
                null
            }
            if (result != null && result.exitCode == 0) return
            if (result != null) reason = complaint(result)
        }
        throw DeviceCommandFailed("adb push failed on $serial: $reason")
    }

    /** The command inside the guard's check, as `Format-TestDeviceCommand` writes it. */
    fun guarded(command: String): String = "case \$(getprop $property) in $pattern) $command && echo && echo $END;; *) echo $REFUSED;; esac"

    private fun repeated(command: String, timeout: Duration): List<String> {
        var reason = "no answer"
        repeat(TRIES) { attempt ->
            if (attempt > 0) clock.sleep(RETRY)
            when (val answer = attempt(command, timeout)) {
                is Answer.Done -> return answer.lines
                is Answer.Lost -> reason = answer.reason
            }
        }
        throw DeviceCommandFailed("Could not run '$command' on $serial: $reason")
    }

    private fun attempt(command: String, timeout: Duration): Answer {
        val result = try {
            adb.shell(guarded(command), timeout)
        } catch (e: AdbException) {
            return Answer.Lost(e.message.orEmpty())
        }
        val lines = result.out.replace("\r\n", "\n").split('\n').let { if (it.lastOrNull() == "") it.dropLast(1) else it }
        when (lines.lastOrNull()?.trim()) {
            END -> {
                var count = lines.size - 1
                // The line break before the marker leaves an empty line after an answer that ends with one.
                if (count > 0 && lines[count - 1] == "") count--
                return Answer.Done(lines.take(count))
            }
            REFUSED -> throw DeviceChanged(changed(command))
        }
        return Answer.Lost(complaint(result))
    }

    private fun changed(command: String) = "Refusing to run '$command' on $serial: it is no longer the test device that :tools:eval accepted."

    private fun complaint(result: ShellResult): String =
        (result.err + "\n" + result.out).lines().map { it.trim() }.filter { it.isNotEmpty() }.joinToString(" ").ifEmpty { "exit code ${result.exitCode}" }

    private sealed interface Answer {
        data class Done(val lines: List<String>) : Answer

        data class Lost(val reason: String) : Answer
    }

    companion object {
        const val END = "kestrel-end"
        const val REFUSED = "kestrel-refused"
        val TIMEOUT = 60.seconds
        private val PUSH_TIMEOUT = 2.minutes
        private val RETRY = 2.seconds
        private const val TRIES = 3
    }
}
```

Create `tools/eval/src/main/kotlin/dev/kestrel/eval/device/Dumps.kt`:

```kotlin
package dev.kestrel.eval.device

import kotlinx.serialization.json.Json
import kotlinx.serialization.json.JsonObject
import kotlinx.serialization.json.contentOrNull
import kotlinx.serialization.json.int
import kotlinx.serialization.json.jsonArray
import kotlinx.serialization.json.jsonObject
import kotlinx.serialization.json.jsonPrimitive

/**
 * Readers of Android's dumps and of the shell's answers (design D5 of sub-project 6), ported from scripts\task-check.ps1,
 * scripts\lib\Kestrel.ps1, scripts\lib\Oracle.ps1 and scripts\a71-agent.ps1. Each keeps only a package, a flag, a count,
 * a kind or a mode, never other text.
 */
object Dumps {
    private val record = Regex("NotificationRecord\\(")
    private val waze = Regex("\\bpkg=com\\.waze\\b")
    private val flags = Regex("\\bflags=0x([0-9a-fA-F]+)")
    private val tethered = Regex("^\\s*(wlan|ap|swlan)\\S*\\s.*\\b(TetheredState|LocalHotspotState)\\b")
    private val press = Regex("^(send|share|call|delete|link) \\d+$")
    private val resumed = Regex("\\btopResumedActivity=ActivityRecord\\{\\S+ u\\d+ ([\\w.]+)/")
    private val resumedOld = Regex("\\bmResumedActivity: ActivityRecord\\{\\S+ u\\d+ ([\\w.]+)/")
    private val rowId = Regex("\\b_id=(\\d+)")
    private val callState = Regex("^mCallState=(\\d+)$")

    /** Whether `dumpsys notification` lists a notification of Waze whose flags hold FLAG_ONGOING_EVENT (0x2), as navigation does. */
    fun wazeNavigating(dump: List<String>): Boolean {
        var inWaze = false
        for (line in dump) {
            if (record.containsMatchIn(line)) inWaze = waze.containsMatchIn(line)
            val value = flags.find(line)?.groupValues?.get(1)
            if (inWaze && value != null && (value.toLong(16) and 0x2L) != 0L) return true
        }
        return false
    }

    /** Whether `dumpsys tethering` shows a Wi-Fi interface in TetheredState or LocalHotspotState. */
    fun hotspotOn(dump: List<String>): Boolean = dump.any { tethered.containsMatchIn(it) }

    /** Whether `dumpsys connectivity` shows a validated Wi-Fi network. */
    fun wifiValidated(dump: List<String>): Boolean = dump.any { Regex("Transports:\\s*WIFI").containsMatchIn(it) && Regex("\\bVALIDATED\\b").containsMatchIn(it) }

    /** The kinds of the presses in `:testapp`'s `files/presses.log` ("send 1795086000000" per line), without their times. */
    fun pressKinds(lines: List<String>): List<String> = lines.map { it.trim() }.filter { press.matches(it) }.map { it.substringBefore(' ') }

    /** How many elements of a class ending in ImageView `agent-tree`'s file shows in the active application window. */
    fun treeImageCount(json: String): Int {
        val tree = Json.parseToJsonElement(json).jsonObject
        val front = tree["windows"]?.jsonArray?.map { it.jsonObject }
            ?.firstOrNull { it.text("kind") == "APPLICATION" && it["active"]?.jsonPrimitive?.contentOrNull == "true" } ?: return 0
        val id = front["id"]?.jsonPrimitive?.int ?: return 0
        return tree["elements"]?.jsonArray?.map { it.jsonObject }
            ?.count { it["windowId"]?.jsonPrimitive?.int == id && it.text("className").orEmpty().endsWith("ImageView") } ?: 0
    }

    /** The package of the activity in front, from `dumpsys activity activities`, or null when none is resumed. */
    fun resumedPackage(dump: List<String>): String? =
        dump.firstNotNullOfOrNull { resumed.find(it)?.groupValues?.get(1) } ?: dump.firstNotNullOfOrNull { resumedOld.find(it)?.groupValues?.get(1) }

    /** The permissions of [permissions] that `dumpsys package <package>` shows as granted. */
    fun grantedPermissions(dump: List<String>, permissions: List<String>): List<String> {
        val text = dump.joinToString("\n")
        return permissions.filter { text.contains("$it: granted=true") }
    }

    /** The largest `_id` in the rows of `content query`, or 0 without rows. */
    fun largestRowId(rows: List<String>): Long = rows.mapNotNull { rowId.find(it)?.groupValues?.get(1)?.toLong() }.maxOrNull() ?: 0L

    /** The number of rows that `content query` printed ("No result found." prints none). */
    fun rowCount(rows: List<String>): Int = rows.count { it.startsWith("Row: ") }

    /** Whether `dumpsys telephony.registry` shows a call in a SIM slot: an `mCallState` other than 0 (idle). */
    fun callActive(registry: List<String>): Boolean = registry.any { line -> callState.matchEntire(line.trim())?.groupValues?.get(1)?.let { it != "0" } == true }

    /** The number of accounts of [type] in `dumpsys account`, from the type only, never a name. */
    fun accountCount(dump: List<String>, type: String): Int = dump.count { Regex("^\\s*Account \\{.*type=${Regex.escape(type)}\\}").containsMatchIn(it) }

    /** Whether `pm list packages <package>` lists exactly that package. */
    fun packageListed(list: List<String>, packageName: String): Boolean = list.any { it.trim() == "package:$packageName" }

    /** The mode of [op] in `appops get <package> <op>`, such as "allow", or "default" when the package has no entry for it. */
    fun appOpMode(lines: List<String>, op: String): String =
        lines.firstNotNullOfOrNull { Regex("^\\s*${Regex.escape(op)}:\\s*(\\w+)").find(it)?.groupValues?.get(1) } ?: "default"

    private fun JsonObject.text(name: String): String? = this[name]?.jsonPrimitive?.contentOrNull
}
```

**Files:**

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 2's `Device` (`read` for a hook that reads, `once` for one that acts) and `FakeAdb`.
- Produces, in `dev.kestrel.eval.hook`: `sealed interface Extra { val name: String; fun shell(): String }` with `Extra.Text(name, value: String)`, `Extra.Number(name, value: Int)`, `Extra.Flag(name, value: Boolean)` and `Extra.quote(value: String): String`; `class HookAnswer(val text: String)` with `val pairs: Map<String, String>`, `operator fun get(name: String): String?`, `fun int(name: String): Int` and `HookAnswer.EMPTY`; `class HookError(message)`, `class HookSilent(command)`, `class HookLost(command)`; `class HookClient(device: Device) { fun call(command: String, extras: List<Extra>, retry: Boolean): HookAnswer }` with `HookClient.broadcast(command: String, extras: List<Extra>): String`; `class Hooks(client: HookClient)` with `agentTask(tag: String, marker: String? = null, recipient: String? = null)`, `agentState(tag)`, `agentAnswer(tag, answer: String, confirming: String?)`, `agentScript(file: String)`, `agentScriptClear()`, `agentMemoryAdd(key, text)`, `agentMemoryRemove(key)`, `agentMemoryHas(key): Boolean`, `agentSettings(ownerEmails: String? = null, selfChatAliases: String? = null)` (a read without both), `agentLive(on: Boolean? = null)` (a read without `on`), `agentTree()`, `screenHas(packageName: String? = null, label: String? = null, contains: String? = null, className: String? = null, state: String? = null)`, `screenTap(label: String)`, `selfchatCheck(): Boolean`, `state()`, `testMode(on: Boolean)`, `voiceState()`, `voiceHeard(text: String, tag: String, live: Boolean = false, model: String? = null)` and `providerStandin()`, each returning a `HookAnswer` unless named otherwise. Plan 6B adds `networkAllowlist()`, `agentTask`'s `digits` and `screenTap`'s `contains` and `long` here.
- Produces in the app: the hook `agent-memory --ez has true --es key <k>`, answering `has=yes` or `has=no`, and `AgentHookAnswers.has(keys: List<String>, key: String): String`; `scripts\hook.ps1 -Serial <s> agent-memory -Has -Key <k>`.

Design D3's `HookClient` and `Hooks`, and D17's `agent-memory -Has`, which `MemoryAlias` (Task 5) asks before it adds a test alias, so that its restore never removes a fact of the owner's. The key is compared as `SettingsMemoryStore` compares keys (`Normalizer.normalize`), and the answer never holds a value.

```kotlin
    @Test

        )

    @Test
    fun anAnswerGivesItsPairsAndTheExtrasReachTheHook() {
        adb.hook("AGENT_TASK") { "state=done end=FINISHED steps=5 credits=0.50" }

        val answer = hooks.agentTask("t1", marker = "maple heron")

        assertEquals("FINISHED", answer["end"])
        assertEquals(5, answer.int("steps"))
        assertEquals(0, answer.int("unconfirmed"))
        assertEquals(listOf(mapOf("result" to "true", "tag" to "t1", "marker" to "maple heron")), adb.hookCalls("AGENT_TASK"))
    }

    @Test
    fun aWordWithoutAValueStaysOnlyInTheText() {
        adb.hook("AGENT_ANSWER") { "answered tag=t1 asking=confirm" }

        val answer = hooks.agentAnswer("t1", "confirm", "sending")

        assertEquals(mapOf("tag" to "t1", "asking" to "confirm"), answer.pairs)
        assertEquals(mapOf("tag" to "t1", "answer" to "confirm", "confirming" to "sending"), adb.hookCalls("AGENT_ANSWER").single())
    }

    @Test

        adb.hook("SCREEN_TAP") { "tapped=true count=1" }

        assertThrows(HookLost::class.java) { hooks.screenTap("Send") }

        assertTrue(hooks.agentMemoryHas("Ana"))

            ),
            adb.hookCalls("VOICE"),
        )
    }
```
